## GHSA-r6hr-vr92-vv28 · phpcsstandards/phpcsutils

2026-09-29 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `AbstractArrayDeclarationSniff::getActualArrayKey()` method used `eval()` to parse array keys. If an attacker could control the content passed to `eval()`, they could inject arbitrary PHP code, leading to remote code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary PHP code on the server where the `phpcsstandards/phpcsutils` package is used, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/PHPCSUtils/AbstractSniffs/AbstractArrayDeclarationSniff.php
+++ b/PHPCSUtils/AbstractSniffs/AbstractArrayDeclarationSniff.php
@@ -591,8 +593,16 @@ public function getActualArrayKey(File $phpcsFile, $startPtr, $endPtr)
             $content .= $this->tokens[$i]['content'];
         }
 
-        // The PHP_EOL is to prevent getting parse errors when the key is a heredoc/nowdoc.
-        $key = eval('return ' . $content . ';' . \PHP_EOL);
+        try {
+            // The PHP_EOL is to prevent getting parse errors when the key is a heredoc/nowdoc.
+            $key = @eval('return ' . $content . ';' . \PHP_EOL);
+        } catch (Throwable $e) { // phpcs:ignore PHPCompatibility.Interfaces.NewInterfaces.throwableFound
+            // The code didn't evaluate cleanly on PHP >= 7.0. Bow out.
+            return;
+        } catch (Exception $e) {
+            // The code didn't evaluate cleanly on PHP < 7.0. Bow out.
+            return;
+        }
 
         /*
          * Ok, so now we know the base value of the key, let's determine whether it is
```

### Fix summary

The patch wraps the `eval()` call in a `try-catch` block to gracefully handle parsing errors. Instead of executing arbitrary code, the function now returns `null` if the evaluation fails, preventing the RCE.

## GHSA-9qh4-3jw8-366w · electron

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The Electron <webview> tag, when configured to disable Node.js integration, failed to properly propagate this restriction to Web Workers. This allowed an attacker to enable Node.js integration within a Web Worker, bypassing the embedder's security policy.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary Node.js code within the context of the main application process, leading to remote code execution and full control over the user's system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/browser/guest-view-manager.ts
+++ b/lib/browser/guest-view-manager.ts
@@ -55,6 +55,7 @@ function makeWebPreferences(embedder: Electron.WebContents, params: Record<strin
     ['contextIsolation', true],
     ['javascript', false],
     ['nodeIntegration', false],
+    ['nodeIntegrationInWorker', false],
     ['sandbox', true],
```

### Fix summary

The patch explicitly disables 'nodeIntegrationInWorker' in the web preferences for guest views and guest windows. It also ensures that this preference is saved and propagated correctly, preventing Web Workers from gaining unintended Node.js access.

## GHSA-gr2m-v5gq-v685 · electron

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because newly opened windows from a sandboxed top-level document in Electron did not correctly inherit the sandbox restrictions of their opener. The check for sandboxing was incorrectly conditioned on the initiator having a parent frame, which is not always true for top-level documents.

### Impact

An attacker could open a new window from a sandboxed context, and this new window would not be subject to the same security restrictions, potentially allowing it to perform actions that should have been prevented by the sandbox.

### Key diff

```
--- a/shell/browser/api/electron_api_web_contents.cc
+++ b/shell/browser/api/electron_api_web_contents.cc
@@ -1420,7 +1420,7 @@ content::WebContents* WebContents::OpenURLFromTab(
     auto* initiator = static_cast<content::RenderFrameHostImpl*>(
         content::RenderFrameHost::FromID(params.source_render_process_id,
                                          params.source_render_frame_id));
-    if (initiator && initiator->GetParent()) {
+    if (initiator) {
       // Use the initiating document's active sandboxing flag set (its policy
       // container flags), which is what
       // content::WebContentsImpl::CreateWithOpener consults when deciding
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the `&& initiator->GetParent()` condition from the sandbox check. This ensures that the sandboxing flags of the initiating document are always considered when opening new windows, regardless of whether the initiator is an iframe or a top-level document.

## GHSA-qmv3-fv6v-rmhq · electron

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the V8 code cache for sandboxed preload scripts was not adequately scoped. A compromised renderer process could write arbitrary code cache blobs for any preload script, and these blobs were only validated against the source length, not the full source content hash. This allowed a malicious renderer to poison the cache for other frames or even other applications using the same preload script path.

### Impact

An attacker who has compromised a renderer process could inject malicious code into the V8 code cache. This malicious code would then be executed by other, potentially more privileged, renderer processes or frames that load the same preload script, leading to further compromise or privilege escalation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/shell/browser/preload_code_cache.h
+++ b/shell/browser/preload_code_cache.h
-std::vector<uint8_t> Get(const std::string& id,
-                         base::span<const uint8_t> contents);
-void Set(const std::string& id,
-         base::span<const uint8_t> source_hash,
-         base::span<const uint8_t> blob);
+std::vector<uint8_t> Get(const Scope& scope,
+                         const std::string& id,
+                         const SourceHash& source_hash);
+void SetFromRenderer(content::RenderFrameHost* rfh,
+                     const std::string& id,
+                     base::span<const uint8_t> claimed_source_hash,
+                     base::span<const uint8_t> blob);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a 'Scope' object that ties cache entries to a specific BrowserContext, process lock, and frame. It also ensures that the browser, not the renderer, is the authority for which source bytes a cache entry validates against by recording the SHA256 hash of the served preload script. Cache writes from the renderer are now only accepted if they match the browser-recorded hash for that specific frame and preload ID.

## GHSA-6h2x-m376-mqjq · joi

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability was caused by an inefficient regular expression used in Joi's `isoDate()` validation. The regex `.*T.*[+-]\d\d$` contained a greedy quantifier `.*` followed by another `.*` and then a fixed string, which could lead to catastrophic backtracking when processing certain malformed input strings, consuming excessive CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted string to the `Joi.string().isoDate()` validator, causing the application to spend an inordinate amount of time processing the input. This could lead to a denial of service, making the application unresponsive or crashing it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/types/string.js
+++ b/lib/types/string.js
@@ -829,8 +829,8 @@ internals.isoDate = function (value) {
         return null;
     }
 
-    if (/.*T.*[+-]\d\d$/.test(value)) {             // Add missing trailing zeros to timeshift
-        value += '00';
+    if (/T.*[+-]\d\d$/.test(value)) {                // Add missing separator and trailing zeros to timeshift
+        value += ':00';
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the problematic regular expression by removing the leading greedy `.*` quantifier. This change prevents catastrophic backtracking by making the regex more specific and efficient, ensuring that the validation process completes in a timely manner even with malicious inputs.

## GHSA-rcw4-f5rp-g42v · adm-zip

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous decompression-bomb protection mechanism in `adm-zip` could be bypassed. When the `expectedLength` (uncompressed size) of a zip entry was declared as 0, the `maxOutputLength` option for `zlib` was either not set or set to 0, effectively disabling the protection. This allowed an attacker to craft a malicious zip file with a small compressed size but a very large uncompressed size, declaring the uncompressed size as 0, to exhaust system resources during decompression.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted zip file that, when decompressed, would consume excessive memory or CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for the application or system processing the file.

### Key diff

```
--- a/methods/inflater.js
+++ b/methods/inflater.js
-    const option = version >= 15 && expectedLength > 0 ? { maxOutputLength: expectedLength } : {};
+    const maxOutputLength = expectedLength > 0 ? expectedLength : 1;
+    const option = version >= 15 ? { maxOutputLength } : {};
...
-                parts.push(data);
                 total += data.length;
+                if (total > maxOutputLength) {
+                    return fail(Errors.MAX_OUTPUT_EXCEEDED());
+                }
+                parts.push(data);
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that `maxOutputLength` is always at least 1, even if the declared uncompressed size is 0. It also adds a manual check for `total > maxOutputLength` in the asynchronous decompression path, as `zlib`'s streaming API ignores `maxOutputLength`, to consistently enforce the cap and prevent resource exhaustion.

## GHSA-rfgv-xxqx-mfg5 · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the WebSocket connection establishment logic did not correctly handle cases where no subprotocols were requested by the client. Specifically, it would attempt to call `.includes()` on a `null` or `undefined` `requestProtocols` array if no protocols were specified, leading to an unhandled exception and a crash.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted WebSocket handshake request without specifying any subprotocols, causing the server to crash and resulting in a Denial of Service for the application using the undici library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/web/websocket/connection.js
+++ b/lib/web/websocket/connection.js
@@ -192,7 +192,7 @@ function establishWebSocketConnection (url, protocols, client, ws, onEstablish,
         // is specified, the server needs to include the same field and one of
         // the selected subprotocol values in its response for the connection to
         // be established.
-        if (!requestProtocols.includes(secProtocol)) {
+        if (requestProtocols === null || !requestProtocols.includes(secProtocol)) {
           failWebsocketConnection(ws, 'Protocol was not set in the opening handshake.')
           return
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check to ensure that `requestProtocols` is not `null` before attempting to call the `.includes()` method on it. This prevents the application from crashing when a WebSocket handshake is initiated without any specified subprotocols.

## GHSA-g84c-rxfj-3j2c · webpack-dev-middleware

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `webpack-dev-middleware` did not properly validate paths when the `publicPath` configuration option was not terminated with a slash. This allowed an attacker to craft a URL that, after stripping the `publicPath` prefix, contained `..` sequences, enabling them to access files outside the intended `outputPath` directory.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's file system that the Node.js process has access to, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/middleware.js
+++ b/src/middleware.js
@@ -196,6 +196,22 @@ async function getFilenameFromUrl(context, url) {
         pathname.slice(publicPathPathname.length),
       );
 
+      const relative = path.relative(outputPath, filename);
+
+      if (
+        relative === ".." ||
+        relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) ||
+        path.isAbsolute(relative)
+      ) {
+        throw new FilenameError("Forbidden", 403);
+      }
+
       const { assetsInfo } = compilation;
       const { outputFileSystem } =
         /** @type {Compiler & { outputFileSystem: OutputFileSystem }} */
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a new validation step that uses `path.relative` to ensure the resolved filename remains within the `outputPath` directory. If the relative path indicates an attempt to escape the root (e.g., `..`, `../`, or an absolute path), a `FilenameError` with a 403 status is thrown.

## GHSA-j84w-jfhq-vhvj · electron

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `CORS_MISCONFIGURATION→ORIGIN`

### Root cause

Electron's custom protocol handlers for 'file://' and 'http://' did not correctly apply the 'opaque' response type for cross-origin, no-cors requests. This meant that even when a request was intended to be opaque (preventing script access to the response body), the custom handlers would serve the response without this crucial security tag, allowing the renderer to read the content.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass same-origin policy restrictions and read the content of local files (via 'file://') or arbitrary HTTP resources (via 'http://') from a different origin, potentially leading to sensitive data disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/shell/browser/net/electron_url_loader_factory.cc
+++ b/shell/browser/net/electron_url_loader_factory.cc
@@ -315,6 +317,71 @@ class URLPipeLoader : public network::mojom::URLLoader,
   base::WeakPtrFactory<URLPipeLoader> weak_factory_{this};
 };
 
+// Forwards everything to a wrapped client while forcing the delivered response
+// to be tagged as opaque. The file and http sinks build or forward their own
+// response head, so they would otherwise lose the opaque tag that StartLoading
+// computes for cross-origin no-cors loads; interpose here so the tag reaches
+// the renderer no matter which sink serves the response.
+class OpaqueResponseFilter : public network::mojom::URLLoaderClient {
+ public:
+  // Returns a client remote to hand to a sink. Owns itself; deletes on
+  // completion or disconnect.
+  static mojo::PendingRemote<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> Wrap(
+      mojo::PendingRemote<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> destination) {
+    auto* filter = new OpaqueResponseFilter(std::move(destination));
+    mojo::PendingRemote<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> proxy;
+    filter->receiver_.Bind(proxy.InitWithNewPipeAndPassReceiver());
+    filter->receiver_.set_disconnect_handler(base::BindOnce(
+        &OpaqueResponseFilter::DeleteThis, base::Unretained(filter)));
+    return proxy;
+  }
+
+  // disable copy
+  OpaqueResponseFilter(const OpaqueResponseFilter&) = delete;
+  OpaqueResponseFilter& operator=(const OpaqueResponseFilter&) = delete;
+
+  // network::mojom::URLLoaderClient:
+  void OnReceiveEarlyHints(network::mojom::EarlyHintsPtr early_hints) override {
+    destination_->OnReceiveEarlyHints(std::move(early_hints));
+  }
+  void OnReceiveResponse(
+      network::mojom::URLResponseHeadPtr head,
+      mojo::ScopedDataPipeConsumerHandle body,
+      std::optional<mojo_base::BigBuffer> cached_metadata) override {
+    head->response_type = network::mojom::FetchResponseType::kOpaque;
+    destination_->OnReceiveResponse(std::move(head), std::move(body),
+                                    std::move(cached_metadata));
+  }
+  void OnReceiveRedirect(const net::RedirectInfo& redirect_info,
+                         network::mojom::URLResponseHeadPtr head) override {
+    destination_->OnReceiveRedirect(redirect_info, std::move(head));
+  }
+  void OnUploadProgress(int64_t current_position,
+                        int64_t total_size,
+                        OnUploadProgressCallback callback) override {
+    destination_->OnUploadProgress(current_position, total_size,
+                                   std::move(callback));
+  }
+  void OnTransferSizeUpdated(int32_t transfer_size_diff) override {
+    destination_->OnTransferSizeUpdated(transfer_size_diff);
+  }
+  void OnComplete(const network::URLLoaderCompletionStatus& status) override {
+    destination_->OnComplete(status);
+    DeleteThis();
+  }
+
+ private:
+  explicit OpaqueResponseFilter(
+      mojo::PendingRemote<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> destination)
+      : destination_(std::move(destination)) {}
+  ~OpaqueResponseFilter() override = default;
+
+  void DeleteThis() { delete this; }
+
+  mojo::Receiver<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> receiver_{this};
+  mojo::Remote<network::mojom::URLLoaderClient> destination_;
+};
+
 }  // namespace
 
 ElectronURLLoaderFactory::RedirectedRequest::RedirectedRequest(
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `OpaqueResponseFilter` that wraps the `URLLoaderClient` for 'file://' and 'http://' protocol handlers. This filter explicitly sets the `response_type` to `kOpaque` for cross-origin, no-cors requests, ensuring that the browser's renderer applies the necessary security restrictions and prevents script access to the response body.

> → GHSA-ccq9-r5cw-5hwq
> → GHSA-gfc2-9qmw-w7vh
> → GHSA-x6qj-4h56-5rj5
> → GHSA-q78p-hj9h-5466
> → GHSA-vj7q-gjh5-988w
> → GHSA-6x6h-qqr7-855w
> → GHSA-96p9-rh4f-92cf
> → GHSA-jmg2-rcxh-w8q3
> → GHSA-vp8m-p9jh-q5pm

## GHSA-vp8m-p9jh-q5pm · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `CORS_MISCONFIGURATION→ORIGIN`

### Root cause

The `undici` HTTP client library's cache interceptor did not properly isolate cache entries based on the request origin. When a redirect occurred, the `opts.origin` value could be updated to the new origin, but the cache key generation did not consistently use an authoritative origin, leading to a scenario where a response from one origin could be cached and served for a request to a different origin.

### Impact

An attacker could poison the cache with malicious responses from a controlled origin. Subsequent requests to a legitimate origin that share the same cache key (e.g., path and method) would then receive the attacker's cached response, potentially leading to data manipulation, unauthorized access, or other security breaches.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/interceptor/cache.js
+++ b/lib/interceptor/cache.js
-  return dispatch => {
-    return (opts, handler) => {
-      if (arrayIncludes(safeMethodsToNotCache, opts.method)) {
-        // Not a method we want to cache, skip
-        return dispatch(opts, handler)
-      }
+  return (dispatch, interceptorOrigin) => {
+    return (opts, handler) => {
+      const requestOrigin = getInterceptorOrigin(opts, interceptorOrigin)
+      if (!requestOrigin || arrayIncludes(safeMethodsToNotCache, opts.method)) {
+        // We cannot safely cache without an authoritative origin, or this is
+        // not a method we want to cache.
+        return dispatch(opts, handler)
+      }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `kRequestOrigin` symbol to explicitly track the original request's origin and ensures that the cache key generation and origin checks within the cache interceptor consistently use this authoritative origin. It also modifies the `compose` method in the dispatcher to pass an `interceptorOrigin` to the interceptors, ensuring proper origin isolation for caching.

> → GHSA-6x6h-qqr7-855w
> → GHSA-96p9-rh4f-92cf
> → GHSA-jmg2-rcxh-w8q3
> → GHSA-j84w-jfhq-vhvj

## GHSA-w293-vg96-wgc3 · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The `BalancedPool` constructor in undici failed to properly propagate `connect` and `tls` options from its constructor arguments to the underlying `kOptions` object. This meant that any custom TLS certificate validation settings or connection options provided by the user were silently dropped, leading to the use of default, potentially insecure, settings.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially perform a Man-in-the-Middle (MitM) attack against connections made through a `BalancedPool` instance if the user had intended to enforce stricter TLS certificate validation. This could lead to interception or manipulation of sensitive data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/dispatcher/balanced-pool.js
+++ b/lib/dispatcher/balanced-pool.js
@@ -49,7 +49,7 @@ function defaultFactory (origin, opts) {
 }
 
 class BalancedPool extends PoolBase {
-  constructor (upstreams = [], { factory = defaultFactory, ...opts } = {}) {
+  constructor (upstreams = [], { factory = defaultFactory, connect, tls, ...opts } = {}) {
     if (typeof factory !== 'function') {
       throw new InvalidArgumentError('factory must be a function.')
     }
@@ -57,7 +57,9 @@ class BalancedPool extends PoolBase {
     super(opts)
 
     this[kOriginless] = true
-    this[kOptions] = { ...util.deepClone(opts) }
+    if (connect && typeof connect !== 'function') connect = { ...connect }
+    if (tls && typeof tls !== 'function') tls = { ...tls }
+    this[kOptions] = { ...util.deepClone(opts), connect, tls }
     this[kIndex] = -1
     this[kCurrentWeight] = 0
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `BalancedPool` constructor to explicitly extract `connect` and `tls` options from the constructor arguments. It then ensures these options are correctly merged into the internal `kOptions` object, preserving user-defined TLS and connection settings.

## GHSA-j5f4-cc29-5x44 · adm-zip

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The `adm-zip` library was extracting files from untrusted ZIP archives while preserving special Unix file permissions, specifically the SUID (Set User ID) and SGID (Set Group ID) bits. These bits, when set on an executable, cause it to run with the permissions of the file's owner or group, respectively, rather than the user executing it.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious ZIP file containing an executable with SUID/SGID bits set. If an administrator or a privileged process extracts this archive, the planted executable could then be run by a local attacker to gain elevated privileges (e.g., root access).

### Key diff

```
-	            return (_attr || 0) >> 16 & 0xfff;
+	            // lets a crafted zip plant a setuid-root binary when extracting as
+	            // root, a local privilege escalation (GHSA-679w-jf3m-wh39).
+	            return ((_attr || 0) >> 16) & 0o777;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `fileAttr` getter to mask out the SUID (0o4000), SGID (0o2000), and sticky (0o1000) bits from the extracted file permissions. It now only preserves the standard rwxrwxrwx (0o777) permission bits, preventing the preservation of dangerous special permissions from untrusted archives.

## GHSA-vv43-5jgx-7qv8 · electron

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.7 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The Squirrel.Mac update installation process on macOS re-evaluated the target bundle URL at multiple points in the install signal chain. This allowed for a Time-of-Check to Time-of-Use (TOCTOU) race condition where an attacker could manipulate symbolic links after the initial path validation but before the final installation step, redirecting the update to an arbitrary location.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this race condition to install a malicious update into an arbitrary location on the file system, potentially leading to privilege escalation or arbitrary code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/Squirrel/SQRLInstaller.m
+++ b/Squirrel/SQRLInstaller.m
@@ -307,6 +310,48 @@ - (RACSignal *)renameIfNeeded:(SQRLShipItRequest *)request updateBundleURL:(NSUR
 - (RACSignal *)installRequest:(SQRLShipItRequest *)request {
 	NSParameterAssert(request != nil);
 
++	// Resolve the target bundle path exactly once and use the canonical URL for
++	// every step of the install chain. The request is read from disk, so the
++	// path it carries is untrusted; every downstream step that re-resolves it
++	// must see the same location that validation saw.
++	NSURL *resolvedTargetURL = request.targetBundleURL.URLByResolvingSymlinksInPath.URLByStandardizingPath;
++	if (![resolvedTargetURL.path isEqual:request.targetBundleURL.URLByStandardizingPath.path]) {
++		NSDictionary *errorInfo = @{
++			NSLocalizedDescriptionKey: NSLocalizedString(@"Target bundle path is not canonical", nil),
```

### Fix summary

The patch resolves the target bundle path to a canonical URL exactly once at the beginning of the installation process. It rejects requests whose paths are not already canonical and threads this resolved URL through the entire install chain. Additionally, it ensures that the running application's bundle URL is resolved to a canonical path on the write side in SQRLUpdater, so the serialized request always carries a trusted path.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 21 occurrences**

## GHSA-2jfj-6hjv-fm6j · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The undici HTTP client library, when configured with a 'shared' cache, would store and re-serve responses containing `Set-Cookie` headers. This allowed cookies intended for one user to be inadvertently disclosed to another user if they made a request that hit the same cached entry.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially gain access to other users' session cookies or other sensitive information stored in cookies, leading to session hijacking or unauthorized access to user accounts.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/handler/cache-handler.js
+++ b/lib/handler/cache-handler.js
@@ -465,7 +471,10 @@ function deleteCachedValueIfNotModified (statusCode, store, cacheKey) {
  */
 function revalidationResponseDisallowsCachedReuse (cacheType, resHeaders, cacheControlDirectives) {
   return cacheControlDirectives['no-store'] === true ||
-    (cacheType === 'shared' && cacheControlDirectives.private === true) ||
+    (cacheType === 'shared' && (
+      cacheControlDirectives.private === true ||
+      Object.hasOwn(resHeaders, 'set-cookie')
+    )) ||
     (resHeaders.vary ? isInvalidOrWildcardVaryHeader(resHeaders.vary) : false)
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the caching logic to prevent responses containing `Set-Cookie` headers from being stored in or re-served from a 'shared' cache. It explicitly checks for the presence of `Set-Cookie` in response headers and, if found in a shared cache context, either prevents caching or deletes the cached entry.

## GHSA-3xpg-4rpp-hhhm · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `undici` library's decompression handler did not impose a limit on the maximum size of decompressed responses. An attacker could send a highly compressed response, causing the server to allocate excessive memory during decompression, leading to resource exhaustion.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a Denial of Service (DoS) by sending a specially crafted, highly compressed response, causing the server to consume all available memory and crash or become unresponsive.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/interceptor/decompress.js
+++ b/lib/interceptor/decompress.js
@@ -28,20 +54,36 @@ let warningEmitted = /** @type {boolean} */ (false)
  * @typedef {Object} DecompressHandlerOptions
  * @property {number[]|Readonly<number[]>} [skipStatusCodes=[204, 304]] - List of status codes to skip decompression for
  * @property {boolean} [skipErrorResponses] - Whether to skip decompression for error responses (status codes >= 400)
+ * @property {number} [maxSize=67108864] - Maximum decompressed response size in bytes
  */
 
 class DecompressHandler extends DecoratorHandler {
   /** @type {Transform[]} */
   #decompressors = []
+  /** @type {Record<string, string | string[]> | undefined} */
+  #trailers
   /** @type {Readonly<number[]>} */
   #skipStatusCodes
   /** @type {boolean} */
   #skipErrorResponses
+  /** @type {number} */
+  #maxSize
+  /** @type {number} */
+  #decompressedSize = 0
+  /** @type {boolean} */
+  #terminated = false
+  /** @type {boolean} */
+  #inputEnded = false
+
+  constructor (handler, { skipStatusCodes = defaultSkipStatusCodes, skipErrorResponses = true, maxSize = defaultMaxSize } = {}) {
+    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(maxSize) || maxSize < 1) {
+      throw new InvalidArgumentError('maxSize must be a positive integer')
+    }
 
-  constructor (handler, { skipStatusCodes = defaultSkipStatusCodes, skipErrorResponses = true } = {}) {
     super(handler)
     this.#skipStatusCodes = skipStatusCodes
     this.#skipErrorResponses = skipErrorResponses
+    this.#maxSize = maxSize
   }
 
   /**
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `maxSize` option to the `DecompressHandler` to limit the total decompressed size. It adds a `createMaxSizeLimiter` transform stream to the decompression pipeline and checks the decompressed size during data processing, aborting if the limit is exceeded.

## GHSA-pmjh-fq2x-6v4x · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the RetryHandler in undici could attempt to forward a response from a retry attempt to a downstream handler that had already received a response from an earlier attempt. This would replace the downstream body, leaving the original response body pending indefinitely and causing a resource leak.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a denial of service by causing the application to leak resources (response bodies) which would eventually lead to resource exhaustion and application unresponsiveness.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/handler/retry-handler.js
+++ b/lib/handler/retry-handler.js
@@ -95,9 +95,16 @@ class RetryHandler {
     if (this.retryOpts.throwOnError) {
       // Preserve old behavior for status codes that are not eligible for retry
       if (this.retryOpts.statusCodes.includes(statusCode) === false) {
-        this.headersSent = true
-        this.checkpointResponseEnd(headers)
-        this.handler.onResponseStart?.(controller, statusCode, headers, statusMessage)
+        if (this.headersSent) {
+          // The downstream handler already received the response from an
+          // earlier attempt. Forwarding this response would replace the
+          // downstream body and leave the original body pending forever.
+          this.handler.onResponseError?.(controller, err)
+        } else {
+          this.headersSent = true
+          this.checkpointResponseEnd(headers)
+          this.handler.onResponseStart?.(controller, statusCode, headers, statusMessage)
+        }
       } else {
         this.error = err
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check for `this.headersSent` before forwarding a response. If headers have already been sent, it means the downstream handler has already received a response, and instead of forwarding a new response, an error is propagated to prevent the original body from being orphaned.

## GHSA-rx4f-c7p8-82vq · undici

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The original implementation of WebSocketStream did not retain a direct reference to the WritableStreamDefaultController. This meant that if the writable stream was locked (e.g., by a writer), the `abort` method on the WritableStream could not be called directly to error it, leading to a state where the stream could not be properly closed or errored.

### Impact

An attacker could prevent the WebSocketStream from properly closing or erroring its writable stream, potentially leading to resource exhaustion or an unresponsive state in the application, causing a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/web/websocket/stream/websocketstream.js
+++ b/lib/web/websocket/stream/websocketstream.js
@@ -34,9 +34,9 @@ class WebSocketStream {
   /** @type {ReadableStreamDefaultController} */
   #readableStreamController
 
-  // Each WebSocketStream object has an associated writable stream , which is a WritableStream .
-  /** @type {WritableStream} */
-  #writableStream
+  // Retain the controller so the writable stream can be errored while locked.
+  /** @type {WritableStreamDefaultController} */
+  #writableStreamController
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the WebSocketStream to store a reference to the WritableStreamDefaultController. This allows the stream to directly call `error()` on the controller, ensuring that the writable stream can be properly errored even when it is locked, thus preventing the denial of service condition.

## GHSA-w6j9-cwv2-h6wq · PyJWT

2026-09-29 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the PyJWT library did not properly handle malformed RSA JWK data within a JWK Set. When parsing a JWK Set, if one of the RSA JWKs was malformed, the parsing process would abort entirely without catching the specific error, leading to a failure to process any subsequent valid keys.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a malformed RSA JWK within a JWK Set, causing the entire set to fail parsing. This could lead to a denial of service by preventing the application from validating signatures or encrypting/decrypting data using keys from that set.

### Key diff

```
--- a/jwt/api_jwk.py
+++ b/jwt/api_jwk.py
@@ -79,7 +79,12 @@ def __init__(self, jwk_data: JWKDict, algorithm: str | None = None) -> None:
                 f"Unable to find an algorithm for key: {self._jwk_data}",
             ) from None
 
-        self.key = self.Algorithm.from_jwk(self._jwk_data)
+        try:
+            self.key = self.Algorithm.from_jwk(self._jwk_data)
+        except ValueError as error:
+            raise InvalidKeyError(
+                f"Unable to construct key from JWK: {error}"
+            ) from error
 
     @staticmethod
     def from_dict(obj: JWKDict, algorithm: str | None = None) -> PyJWK:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a try-except block around the `self.Algorithm.from_jwk(self._jwk_data)` call. It specifically catches `ValueError` exceptions that occur during key construction and re-raises them as `InvalidKeyError`, ensuring that individual malformed keys are handled gracefully without aborting the entire JWK Set parsing process.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 192 occurrences**

## GHSA-jh5r-qr3c-85q8 · laravel/framework

2026-09-29 · PHP · **LOW** 3.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Laravel debug page was using a JavaScript library (Tippy.js) to display tooltips. The `data-tippy-content` attribute was populated with potentially untrusted source code (`$source`) without proper HTML escaping, and the Tippy.js configuration allowed HTML content (`allowHTML: true`) by default for this attribute.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious JavaScript into the debug page, which would execute in the browser of anyone viewing the debug page. This could lead to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Illuminate/Foundation/resources/exceptions/renderer/scripts.js
+++ b/src/Illuminate/Foundation/resources/exceptions/renderer/scripts.js
@@ -10,10 +10,19 @@
 
 tippy('[data-tippy-content]', {
     arrow: false,
-    allowHTML: true,
+    allowHTML: false,
     animation: 'shift-away',
     delay: [300, 0],
     duration: 200,
     theme: 'laravel',
 });
 
+tippy('[data-tippy-html-content]', {
+    arrow: false,
+    allowHTML: true,
+    animation: 'shift-away',
+    delay: [300, 0],
+    duration: 200,
+    theme: 'laravel',
+});
 
--- a/src/Illuminate/Foundation/resources/exceptions/renderer/components/formatted-source.blade.php
+++ b/src/Illuminate/Foundation/resources/exceptions/renderer/components/formatted-source.blade.php
@@ -19,5 +19,5 @@
     language="php"
     truncate
     class="text-xs min-w-0"
-    data-tippy-content="{{ $source }}"
+    data-tippy-html-content="{{ $source }}"
 />
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `data-tippy-html-content` attribute for content that is explicitly intended to be HTML. The default `data-tippy-content` attribute is now configured with `allowHTML: false`, ensuring that its content is treated as plain text and preventing XSS. The `formatted-source.blade.php` component was updated to use the new `data-tippy-html-content` attribute.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 119 occurrences**

## GHSA-253c-mchw-3w2r · markdown-it

2026-09-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The original implementation of the linkify rule in markdown-it used `arrayReplaceAt` within a loop to modify the token array. When many links were present in a markdown block, this repeated array manipulation (which involves creating new arrays) led to quadratic time complexity, causing significant performance degradation and blocking the event loop.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a markdown input with a large number of link-like structures, causing the application processing the markdown to consume excessive CPU resources and block the event loop for an extended period, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
-        blockTokens[j].children = tokens = arrayReplaceAt(tokens, i, nodes)
+        replacements.push({ index: i, nodes })
...
+      blockTokens[j].children = newTokens
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the token replacement logic. Instead of modifying the array in place within the loop, it collects all necessary replacements and then constructs a completely new token array in a single pass. This changes the complexity from quadratic to linear, resolving the performance bottleneck.
